No, distance from f6 to e8: f->e (1), 6->8 (2). That's a knight move!
So the King on e8 is in check by the Knight on f6.
Therefore, I MUST move out of check.
Legal moves that remove the check:
1. Capture the knight: `f7f6`.
2. Move the king: `e8f8` or `e8d8`.

Comparing `f7f6` vs `e8f8/e8d8`:
`f7f6` captures the knight, removing the threat and gaining material.
`e8f8` or `e8d8` just moves the king, leaving the knight to continue attacking.

Capturing the piece checking you is almost always the best move unless it leads to mate or loses more material.
If `f7f6`, is the pawn on f6 defended?
White pawn is on e6. A pawn on e6 attacks f7 and d7. It does NOT protect f6.
Is there any other piece protecting f6?
Rooks are at c1 and h1. Queen is at a7.
Queen at a7 cannot reach f6.
Rooks at c1/h1 cannot reach f6.
So `f7f6` captures the knight for free.

Therefore, `f7f6` is the strongest move.
